Let me try a different approach:\n\n1. First populate the transformers module cache manually by running a quick Python command\n2. Or use `local_files_only=True` parameter\n\n\n3. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_016cHF34LHyogn1QorUMJfEi","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Same issue — it can't find the processor in the cache. The issue is that the transformers 5.5.3 `cached_files` looks for files in the HF cache (under hub/models--...) not in the transformers module cache. The model loads fine because `from_pretrained` for the model class already resolved to the local directory. But `AutoProcessor.from_pretrained` goes through a different code path that checks the HF cache.\n\nThe core problem: The spatial model was downloaded using `snapshot_download(local_dir=...)` which puts files directly in the target directory but does NOT populate the HF cache's `hub/models--openvla--openvla-7b-finetuned-libero-spatial/` structure (snapshots, refs, blobs). \n\nThe model loads fine because it finds the files directly, but the processor needs to resolve through the cache. I'm considering a few approaches: properly structuring the HF cache directories, using `local_files_only=True` on the processor call, leveraging the object model's processor instead, or setting up a symlink to trick the cache lookup.\n\nActually, the quickest solution is to just enable network access by setting the offline flags to 0 and sourcing the network proxy before running—that way the processor can download the module from HuggingFace, which should be a small transfer. Let me try {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01Nr6SSgKARD4g1oBzkeLum3","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Still fails.
